**2026-11-02 portable JDK identity direction:** The user requires the toolchain lock, not an absolute Java installation path, to select Java. The tracked schema-4 `jdk_pins` already contains portable major/vendor/version/build/flavor and platform URL/SHA-512 (the selected dev 1.19.2 JBRSDK17 pin has no absolute path). The current `jdk` CLI only lists discovered installations; it lacks add/remove/show and a machine-local logical-ID preference registry. Implement `jdk add/list/remove/show` as a local locator mapping an ID such as `jbr17` to a home, but registration alone must never override the exact locked vendor/build/platform/archive/content proof. Keep an exact immutable pin/spec in tracked metadata even when exposing a thin ID; do not reduce a strict lock to a mutable Java-major alias. `named_sdk_identity` currently includes absolute home and executable paths alongside hashes, and snapshot reuse compares those paths; this causes an unnecessary namespace miss when identical checked JBR bytes move. Split portable content/lock cache identity from physical path admission, retained reader handles and path-bound historical receipts. Test same exact pin/bytes at two safe homes as the same immutable native project/cache key and changed pin/content or unsafe/misdirected path as refusal. Path-bound successful-build snapshots and hotswap launch receipts may remain conservatively location-sensitive until the current-Java launch contract is proved; do not call those cross-path reuse. Explicit/unpinned legacy Java remains conservative until its equivalence proof is defined. Gradle launcher Java and task toolchain Java are separate: Gradle's installed-JDK discovery and `languageVersion=17/21` select compatible metadata rather than SFM's exact JBR build. Direct normal wrappers still choose pinned launcher Java where supported; do not assume newer daemon-JVM criteria exist in older wrappers. Source: `src/toolchain_lockfile_schema/version/v4.rs`, `src/cli/jdk`, `src/jar_build/engine_named_compile.rs`, `src/jar_build/build_snapshot_receipt.rs`; Gradle official toolchain and daemon documentation at https://docs.gradle.org/current/userguide/toolchains.html and https://docs.gradle.org/current/userguide/gradle_daemon.html. This adds a bounded Rust identity/CLI implementation gate, not renewed Gradle shutdown or home/cache work.

## How to update this plan

Use `[ ]` not started, `[~]` in progress, `[x]` complete, `[!]` blocked only with exact reason and unblock condition. Update the heading and completion evidence together. A phase closes only when all its tasks are `[x]`. Record commits, commands, results and exceptions beside their task, not in a detached log. Follow the first unfinished item, with one heavy desktop build/GameTest job at a time.

## Guidance, findings and design requirements

`U` records the user's instruction or preference. `F` records source or measured findings, which are evidence rather than user instructions. `R` records proposed engineering obligations derived from the requested outcome and findings. A later reviewer may challenge an F or R without rewriting what Teamy asked for.

### Authoritative user guidance

## Purpose and scope

**Observable end state:** This Git checkout can bootstrap from frozen inputs, then build and run multiple projections without repeatedly storing separate physical copies of compatible Minecraft/dependency preparations or accumulating successful scratch and unchanged-run derivatives. Gradle remains independent. Allocated bytes across *old and new SFM-owned roots together* fall by a reviewed, evidence-derived substantial target after separately approved retirement. A final ledger shows exactly which projections resolved, compiled, packaged, launched and ran applicable tests.

**Current implementation scope (authorized 2026-10-28):** Rust shared-input/stage ownership, Gradle relative access only where compatible, retained-output integrity, scratch/run lifetime, frozen closure, tests and scoped local commits. Existing-recipe missing lock pins may be corrected with verified provenance, version and digest. Optional, explicitly selected cross-worktree sharing and report-only prune candidates remain in scope. **Separate approval still required:** migrating existing cache data, creating a worktree or filesystem sharing link, deleting/retiring existing data, adding genuinely new dependencies or upgrades, installation, push or release. Exclude a custom opaque CAS unless a gate proves necessary; ZIP normalization is optional. Do not move Gradle `build/`, support copied standalone projections, enable cross-worktree sharing automatically, alter Prism/tracked-server internals, or retire plain 4.34. Version-specific Gradle file pruning is separate later work.

## Optional worktree sharing and pruning boundaries

The proposed logical path remains `<checkout>/sfm-toolchain-data/` for **every** worktree. An isolated worktree has a real directory there. An explicitly approved sharing worktree may instead place one directory link **at that root only**, pointing to a common physical cache outside the projection trees. Rust and generated Gradle resolve the same project-relative literal; neither discovers a Git root dynamically or requires a new preferred-directory indirection. Existing persistent/argument overrides are a compatibility and explicit-selection gate at G3, not a silent way to choose sharing. No link or target exists by implication from this plan. A directory link is not a license to follow links below the root, move projection snapshots, or mix mutable world/session files.

On Windows, `mklink /d` creates a directory symbolic link and `mklink /j` creates a junction; junctions can target another **local** volume but not a mapped network volume. The choice is a G7 platform/permission/relocation decision: a relative directory symlink may make a nearby target portable, while a junction is appropriate only for a verified local target and may bind its location. Neither is created automatically, and no OS privilege or security setting change is part of this plan. [Microsoft mklink](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/windows-commands/mklink); [Microsoft junction semantics](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/fileio/hard-links-and-junctions).

Record both the checkout's **logical alias** and the resolved **physical target** (canonical path, file identity/volume and ownership marker) at selection, publication, accounting and cleanup. The target must be independently owned and durable, not under a worktree's disposable build or a directory that worktree cleanup could remove. Two aliases to one target count as **one** allocation, including during old/new migration peak accounting. Shared artifact locks/read holds and atomic publication must be under the physical target and keyed by the same complete relevant-input recipe across worktrees; incompatible schema/tool recipes occupy different namespaces and never overwrite. Hold or revalidate the admitted physical directory identity across publication/reader lifetimes, failing closed if an alias is switched after preflight. Full source/lock validation remains per checkout. A resource newly required by another worktree is additive. Project compilation, snapshots, worlds, options, session state and diagnostic ownership remain checkout/instance-local. Existing `native_cache_root.rs` and `paths/cache.rs` reparse refusals mean this is a planned narrow change, **not currently working behavior**.

For pruning, an exact current-checkout expected set can start with portable frozen lock paths, authenticated version-manifest downloads and verified asset-index/object references. A derived stage may be expected exactly only when its complete recipe inputs and schema are known from the planner or a validated receipt; lazy runtime inputs, optional profiles, missing/offline owners and old schema entries are conservative/unknown. Define `existing physical entries − expected by this checkout` as **report-only candidates**. It does **not** prove global orphanhood. `git worktree list` is a useful local input but cannot establish ownership of other clones, offline worktrees or preserved rollback artifacts. A shared target's deletion decision would require a complete, explicit owner inventory (including unavailable/retired owners and retained receipts), expected-set union, exclusion of active readers/publishers, a common maintenance lock or equivalent quiescence protocol, last-moment identity/receipt recheck and a separate exact-list approval. If that evidence is incomplete, retain the entry; do not add a persistent owner registry merely for convenience. Any future deletion operates on individually verified physical entries under the canonical target, never a recursive traversal through a checkout symlink/junction.

## Confirmed geometry and design gates

The Git checkout root is `D:\Repos\Minecraft\SFM\worktrees\sfm-main-source-projection`, whose `.git` is a **file**. `D:\Repos\Minecraft\SFM` contains multiple repositories, so it is not the checkout root. Existing catalog projection path `platform/minecraft/projections/<name>/mc-<version>` reaches the checkout root with **five** `..` components (`../../../../..`), verified with an existing extended 1.19.2 projection; `platform/minecraft` reaches it via `../..`. Proposed checkout-local `sfm-toolchain-data/` is not created or selected and needs a root `.gitignore` rule. Keep it separate from Cargo `target/` and Gradle `build/`.

| Gate | Decide before downstream work | Acceptance |
| --- | --- | --- |
| G1 physical target | Measure allocated bytes, old+new physical roots, file IDs/links and peak writes; count any shared target once even when multiple worktree paths point to it. Choose numerical **substantial** reduction and unchanged-build/run growth budgets from verified candidates, not invented percentages. | No success claim without measured before/after and reviewed target. |
| G2 stage read-sets | Record ordered inputs, patches, mapping, loader/tool/JDK/platform and mutable readers for MCP, Forge, remap and NeoForm. | Same-key hit; relevant-input miss; unrelated-source hit; shared file never mutated. |
| G3 root/cutover | Confirm baked-in relative literals and one effective override rule in Rust/Gradle; classify path-bound receipts. | Rust uses the verified checkout root; Gradle launches independently with pinned Java. Optional Gradle cache unification is deferred. Existing D: stays untouched until separate approval. |
| G4 Gradle/parity | Select only compatible shared artifacts; retain Gradle output and plain 4.34/4.35 historical bytes. | Gradle works without Rust; release source comparison unchanged. |
| G5 lifetime/integrity | Define reader holds, atomic publish, failure evidence, asset-index/output digests, partial-entry recovery. | Corruption, crash, concurrent readers and 32-entry saturation fail closed and recover. |
| G6 applicability | Freeze per-key/profile/operation required, N/A, external and blocked rows before broad execution. | 74 keys classified with source-backed reasons; no zero-selector or failure counted N/A. |
| G7 optional link design | Decide whether a fixed-path filesystem link is the easiest/best safe sharing option, then document real-directory versus approved root-level link, Windows symlink versus junction/platform equivalent, target ownership/access/capacity and admission rules. No link is created to close this design gate. | Reviewed design and approval/test sequence specify how wrong/broken/misdirected/nested/switched links will fail closed and how two aliases will use one physical lock namespace. Runtime proof belongs to M2.1/M3/M5. |
| G8 prune reachability | Decide exact direct-resource enumeration, conservative derived/lazy set, owner-union evidence and active-reader/quiescence boundary. | One-checkout difference is candidates only; deletion is separately approved and impossible when owner/identity evidence is incomplete. |
| G9 source artifacts | Inventory source-provider, JDK/classfile stub, platform transformed-source and symbol-index inputs/outputs, namespaces, integrity and reader lifetimes. Decide immutable publication or private materialization for each, including repair under a linked root. | Warm source/index reads verify the artifact they consume; a concurrent reader cannot observe remove-before-rename; source trees and indexes enter G8 reachability without deleting active evidence. |
| G10 build-tool opportunities | Complete per-target producer/consumer JAR inventory and rank native Rust substitutions by semantic parity, mapping correctness, historical output fidelity, provenance/licensing and measured cost. | Each candidate has a bounded experiment/decision, or explicit deferral; no substitution is a prerequisite to M5/M6 absent a demonstrated correctness blocker. |
| G11 validation window | Designate one integration coordinator, enumerate source/config/generated/cache read and write closures for each validation command, and define freeze/thaw, drift, child-process and evidence rules before parallel mutation. | Coherent worker checkpoint; pre/post input identity matches; no concurrent writer to source or tested fixtures/cache/config; stale/interrupted results are invalidated; unrelated user edits preserved. |

## Support populations and the G6 applicability freeze

The catalog has **74 keys**, the prior lock audit covered **60 materialized locks**, and the core release/extended/dev population is **50 targets**. These denominators are not interchangeable. MC 1.21.0 catalog keys use `minecraft_version: "1.21"`; never infer a missing version from that alias.

| Key family | Count | Required before G6 can close | Operation proposal to confirm per key at G6 |
| --- | ---: | --- | --- |
| Plain 4.34.0 and 4.35.0 | 20 | Historical source/resource equality, frozen lock and declared recipe availability for each key. | Native compile/package where the declared historical recipe supports it; ordinary launch only where that route is supported. Added FE fixture/guard GameTests are N/A because plain releases intentionally exclude them. A failing supported recipe is a blocker, not N/A. |
| Extended red/green | 20 | Frozen lock/source and exact paired baseline/fixed behavior for every MC target. | Native compile, JAR, server launch and one selected portable FE GameTest per key, with red second consumer 0 FE and green 100,000 FE after drain. Graphical inspection is a separately identified representative 1.21.1 pair, not a proxy for all-version runtime. |
| `sfm-dev` | 10 | Frozen lock/source, infrastructure-guard coverage and declared dependency profiles. | Native compile/JAR and applicable selected headless guard tests per MC target; client-only tests need a client receipt. The dev26 AE2 zero-match requires an explicit applicability decision, not an inferred pass. |
| Other issue-610/compatibility variants | 24 | Classify every key's declared features, source lock, intended operation and dependency profile. | Source render/lock check for all; compile/JAR/launch/GameTest only for variants whose declared profile and route support them. Record the exact reason and evidence for each N/A. Do not silently promote these into the 50-target core. |

G6 produces a frozen ledger with one row per **key × declared profile × operation**, status `required`, `N/A`, `external`, or `blocked`, reason, source declaration and expected proof. It freezes the set **before** M4's broad sweep. A zero-selector, missing dependency, code error or untested supported route cannot become N/A merely to make the ledger green. Adding a required route after the freeze is a change-control decision with owner, rationale, capacity estimate and new acceptance row.

**G6 source applicability scope frozen, 2026-10-29; execution remains open:** `scripts/report-projection-applicability.py` schema 3 classifies all 74 catalog keys into 1,540 key × profile × operation rows. Each row records status, lock SHA-256 when materialized, source basis, route owner, expected proof and an exact selector/minimum count where applicable. Task evidence `action-services-evidence/sfm-g6-frozen-scope-20261029.json` has SHA-256 `C8754D4776639F9EF3DE40147B3911D4ACDB398032D7A1C07196F3C56B409250`; seven focused script tests pass. The frozen classification is 452 required, 109 blocked, 185 external and 794 N/A rows; this is **not** 452 passing tests. There are 56 materialized locks: all 50 core keys plus six issue-610 keys. Eighteen portable keys have no materialized lock and account for 108 blocked rows; dev26's previously zero-selected exact AE2 test is the other blocked row. Synthetic `legacy-schema-v2` and `unmaterialized` profile labels preserve older/missing-lock identity until real profiles are known. The ordinary plain 4.34 catalog client route is required without smoke; plain 4.35's Gradle client route is external. Infrastructure witnesses are four on 26.1.2 and five on older targets, with expected pass/fail outcomes recorded individually; progress selection covers the resource test and seven JDT scenarios. `SFMGameTestDiscovery.filterSelectedTests` rejects zero matches, so source-derived minimum counts still require actual runtime receipts. Gradle-without-Rust, Prism and tracked-server ownership is explicit and separate. The earlier 60-lock audit and 50-target core matrix remain distinct denominators. G6 scope is frozen, but missing locks, the AE2 selector defect, native/Gradle/client execution, and external-route receipts remain M4 blockers; none became N/A merely to close the scope decision. New required rows follow change control.

Gradle-without-Rust has its own Forge/NeoForge smoke rows (M2.2); its local compilation store is not a Rust-stage reuse claim. The named historical-JAR route has four exact 4.34 Forge recipe rows and its own cache restriction; classify whether a relevant command exercises it. Prism and tracked servers are separately owned external routes: record their boundary and evidence needed to avoid an SFM-owned cache claim, but do not redesign their internals. Mutable worlds, options and evidence are never stage-cache rows.

## Source and build-tool artifact inventory (static sample; M1.5 completes all targets)

`sfm-propagate-changes dependency source configure/acquire/search/provider` and `symbol index refresh/show` are separate from `source project ...`. Search inspects materialized trees and can report incomplete providers; refresh may acquire selected sources. A successful source search is not proof that all compile/runtime JARs were resolved. The following rows are source-backed examples, not an all-version manifest. M1.5 must join each projection's lock artifact records, purpose/role, loader/MCP configuration and actual invocation/classpath before claiming coverage. Record URL/repository, digest, portable cache path, producer, JDK, mappings/namespace and exact consumer for each row; a named JAR alone cannot establish its role.

| Producer/input | Verified acquisition and role | Output, reuse boundary and source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Maven binary and upstream `sources` classifier | `dependency_source_configure_cli.rs` selects source provider; `source_maven.rs` authenticates the locked source archive digest. Artifacts carry coordinate, repository ID, URL, BLAKE3 and `$sfm-cache/maven/...` in sampled dev 1.19.2 lock. A source classifier is optional source text, not automatically a compile dependency. | `source_cache.rs` uses coordinate plus exact archive hash for archive/tree. `source_archive.rs` extracts with path/entry checks but replacement removes the old tree first; extracted file inventory/reader hold is not proven. |
| Pinned Git source and generated classfile/JDK source | `source_git.rs` uses canonical HTTPS bare repository and exact commit, streams a plain tree; `dependency_classfile_stubs.rs` generates Minecraft stubs from authenticated version JSON; JDK source archive is separately keyed. | `source_cache.rs` keys Git remote+commit, stubs by MC/version JSON/generator and JDK by release/archive/parser. Git tree marker plus configured roots is not a full payload audit, and local Git-source cleanup discovers only local worktrees. |
| Dependency binary decompile fallback | `source_decompile.rs` invokes locked `org.vineflower:vineflower:1.12.0` with Java `-jar ... --folder`; identity includes binary/decompiler hashes, Java runtime identity and fixed options/no-external-mappings policy. The sampled lock pins its Maven URL/BLAKE3. | Decompiled tree keyed by binary hash+recipe fingerprint; `.sfm-decompile-fingerprint` and roots check reuse, not every output byte. `source_provider.rs` selects it as one provider, not a synonym for MCP ForgeFlower. |
| Minecraft/loader transformed sources and mappings | `engine_sources.rs` selects Forge `forge/<mc>/sources/combined-deobfuscated.jar` or NeoGradle `neoform/<mc>/classes/gameSourcesWithNeoForge.jar` in the plan's preparation cache; `source_output_filetree.rs` extracts adjacent `.filetree`. `source_identifier_remapper.rs` already rewrites Java identifiers in Rust for one Forge preparation stage. | These are pipeline-derived JARs/trees, presently projection-local; keys need the actual loader, mapping, patch, tool and JDK read-set. Mapping namespaces and source lookup cannot be inferred from the output name. |
| Search and symbol index | `dependency source search`, `symbol index refresh/show`, `symbol serve`, `symbol show-definition` and `symbol list-usages` read selected roots. `java_analysis/dependency_index_identity.rs` rejects machine-local path identity inputs; `dependency_index_store.rs` validates payload bytes, uses schema identity and publication/reader controls. | Index/manifest is a separate derived artifact. Treat source roots, shards, live-query lock and active search/server readers separately from compile outputs; do not generalize index integrity to Maven/Git/decompile trees. |
| Forge/MCP build tools | `engine_plan.rs::add_mcp_tool_coordinates` reads MCP config with fallbacks; sampled dev 1.19.2 lock pins ForgeFlower 1.5.605.9, SpecialSource 1.11.0, DiffPatch 2.0.12 and FART 0.1.22 with Maven repository URLs/BLAKE3. `engine_named_compile.rs` pins four version-specific 4.34 recipes. `nfrt_child_identity_supplement.rs` records NeoForm's separate ordered Vineflower 1.11.2/plugin 0.1.5 classpath. | Tool-invocation inputs, not SFM runtime dependencies merely because they are JARs. Keep original recipe and historical 4.34 output fidelity; distinguish MCP ForgeFlower, dependency-source fallback Vineflower 1.12.0 and NeoForm's Vineflower classpath. |
| Generation/compile/runtime libraries | `engine_sources.rs` resolves ANTLR tool classpath separately from project compile classpath, adding loader dev JAR, MC libraries, Forge userdev libraries, declared dependencies, remapped dependency JARs, annotations and ANTLR entries in order. The sampled lock pins ANTLR4 4.9.1, Mixin 0.8.5 and its processor. Source-built Teamy Vox Java has a separate Git source/recipe/hash contract. | `dependencies.kind=tool` misses tool records in `artifacts`; purpose and actual invocation decide role. Mutable project classes/resources, runtime worlds and evidence stay outside immutable source/tool caches. |

**Bounded native-tool assessment:** Already implemented Rust identifier remapping and Java parsing/indexing should be measured for reuse before adding another external tool. Candidates such as ForgeFlower/Vineflower decompilation, FART/SpecialSource remapping, DiffPatch and ANTLR generation require per-version mapping/namespace parity, exact or justified semantic output comparison, error behavior, tool/JDK/provenance and licensing review. Maven/Gradle/NeoForm recipe JARs are not interchangeable with a Rust command solely because they perform a similar operation. G10 records benefits and a decision for each candidate; experimental replacement is deferred unless a correctness gate demonstrates it is necessary for cache migration. Plain 4.34 historic recipes stay intact.

## Mandatory source-stable validation window

**Integration coordinator:** the active SFM task owner holds the validation lane, assigns one writer per file and maintains the checkpoint/receipt ledger. Parallel research and review may continue read-only. A worker saying “ready” means its own edits are saved, coherent and its generators/test helpers have stopped; it does **not** claim a successful build. The coordinator may run an initial stable baseline build if compiling readiness is unknown. Cargo's target-directory output lock, per-key artifact locks and reader holds protect their own artifacts, **not** this source closure.

For **every** Cargo `check`, `test`, `build` or `run`, and each native source/Gradle/GameTest acceptance command, use **EDIT → QUIESCE → FROZEN VALIDATE → RELEASE**:

1. **EDIT:** assign exact files and generated-output ownership. Integrate coherent worker checkpoints in dependency order: shared API/schema/manifest/lock/build-script changes first, then consumers and generated inputs, then validation. No agent other than the coordinator stages or commits. Review scoped diffs, the index and untracked relevant inputs; never use blanket staging or reset another owner's edits. A failed worker leaves its edits visible and marked incomplete, not silently reverted.
2. **QUIESCE:** stop all source, formatter, codegen, IDE auto-save and fixture/config writers in the command's input closure. Confirm prior child compilers, JVMs, test servers and helpers exited; timeout, cancellation or coordinator failure does not implicitly release a lease. Record exact command, working directory, tool/binary hash, JDK, environment variables that affect inputs, persisted cache/config root and frozen dependency preconditions. Enumerate the actual read closure: Rust crate/path dependencies/proc macros, `build.rs`, `include_bytes!`/templates, `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock`/`.cargo` config; for native/Gradle add catalog/features, authored/generated lock/source/resources, Gradle scripts/wrapper, mappings and selected artifact digests. A generated input must be complete and quiescent before its consumer; a generator itself gets its own writer lease and validation phase. Record HEAD plus path/byte-hash inventory of relevant staged, unstaged and untracked content, not just `git status`.
3. **FROZEN VALIDATE:** coordinator holds an exclusive **source-input writer exclusion** for that closure through result collection. No worker, user-facing helper, formatter, generator or concurrent CLI may edit it. Give mutable effects a separate lease: tests/commands that write the same fixture, cache entry, generated project, persisted configuration, installed CLI or runtime output serialize unless isolation is proven. `cargo test` may run tests concurrently inside one process, so shared fixture/cache writers must be isolated or run serially. Declared output paths may be written by the owner command, but never mistaken for frozen inputs. One heavy desktop command at a time remains the resource rule, separate from source stability.
4. **RELEASE:** wait for every owned process/child and relevant file handle to exit, collect exit/selected-test counts/logs and output artifact identities, then rehash/recheck the frozen input closure and configuration. Matching before/after hashes supplement, but do not replace, writer exclusion (edit-and-revert is possible). If identity drifted or an unowned writer appeared, mark the result **stale**, preserve the evidence, quiesce again and revalidate; do not overwrite a concurrent user edit. Only then thaw and allow queued edits. Attribute a receipt to a later scoped commit only if its relevant source/lock/config byte identity still matches the tested checkpoint; record any post-test change requiring rerun.

**Failure and rollback:** if a worker or coordinator fails, preserve dirty files, staged/index state, cache receipts and diagnostics; identify owned process trees before stopping them and confirm termination before thaw. Do not call `git reset --hard`, broad clean or recursive cache deletion. Repair or reverse only identified own hunks after diff review, leaving user edits intact. Interrupted/partial tests are unvalidated. Recovery resumes at QUIESCE with an explicit new input identity. Global `cache root set/unset`, `install.ps1`, source generation and runtime session changes are serialized writes with their own receipts; an installed binary is never assumed to match the current source build.

**Focused omissions audit (source inspection, not a completed validation):**

| Boundary | Earlier plan coverage | Missing execution rule now required |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Disjoint source/API files | One writer per file and one heavy job | Whole-crate Cargo reads path dependencies, proc macros, build scripts, embedded templates and `Cargo.lock`; no edits anywhere in that closure during any Cargo check/test/build/run. `forge_preparation_inputs.rs:696–801` embeds broad producer files and lock bytes. |
| Generated project and locks | Frozen dependency and source receipts | Source generation is a writer; freeze generated Java/resources, catalog/features, authored/generated lock and Gradle scripts before the consumer phase. `CatalogOwnedProject::recheck` is a checkpoint, not a continuous lock. |
| Shared cache, fixtures and configuration | Per-key artifact locks/held readers | Coordinate test caches, fixtures, root setting and native/Gradle output writers separately. One Cargo test command can run colliding tests concurrently; use isolated fixtures or serialized test threads for shared state. |
| Validation and installed binary | `check-all.ps1`, `install.ps1` end gates | Record source closure and exact CLI/JDK/env/cache state. `check-all.ps1` runs Cargo build/tests; `install.ps1` installs a binary. Only the coordinator performs the install after a stable pass and verifies its hash. |
| Worker interruption and evidence | Cancellation/partial-entry handling for artifacts | A failed worker, timed-out child or uncollected process is not a thaw. Preserve partial diagnostics and staged/unstaged identity; revalidate against a stable checkpoint. |
| Integration and user edits | Scoped commits and preserved unrelated files | Coordinator alone stages/commits after index review; correlate receipts with exact dirty bytes or commit, detect user edits and invalidate stale evidence without reset or overwrite. |

The prior cache design gates G1–G10, separate M5/M6 approvals and one heavy-resource lane remain valid. This protocol adds the missing **source/test-input** serialization; it does not replace artifact locks, reader holds or parallel read-only investigation.

## Execution order, parallelism and change control

M0 checkpoint -> M1 physical/read-set/applicability/reachability gates -> M2 root, optional-link and Gradle contracts -> independently closable M3 producer/integrity/lifetime/reporting slices -> M4 frozen final-source workload closure -> separately approved M5 cutover -> separately approved M6 retirement/savings -> M7 closure. G7 closes the **design** choice without a link. M2.3's dry-reviewed guide must close **before** any approved link/worktree creation for sharing; M2.1 code can be prepared earlier, but its link tests wait for the guide and separate creation approval. M3.5 asset-index and M3.6 run-output integrity can implement after G5 without waiting for every shared producer; M3.7 snapshots follows G3; M3.8 session retention follows the run/snapshot ownership decisions. M3.9 candidate reporting follows G8 and is **read-only**, so it does not wait for retirement. The heavy desktop build/GameTest lane remains **single-job and serialized**. No new worktree or link is implied; future linked-worktree tests themselves require prior approval.

| Track / owner boundary | Exclusive files while active | Prerequisite and integration order |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A1 Forge/MCP | `jar_build/forge_preparation_{cache,inputs,producers,capture}.rs` and related tests | G2/M2.1; integrate M3.1 before M3.4 touches the same cache. |
| A2 remap | `jar_build/{dependency_remap_cache,dependency_deobf_producer,local_dependency_cache}.rs` and related tests | G2/M2.1; independent of A1 implementation until shared interface integration. |
| A3 NeoForm/native | `jar_build/native_cache_root.rs` and G2-identified NeoForm producer/consumer files | G2/M2.1; preserve current held-reference behavior. |
| B Gradle fallback | Generated Gradle templates and generator mappings | M2 relative contract; no A files; integrate with plain source equality. |
| C1 asset/run integrity | `container_build/minecraft_asset_index.rs` for M3.5; `jar_build/engine_run.rs` for M3.6 | G5 contracts; independently implementable before all producer slices. |
| C2 snapshot/session | `jar_build/{build_snapshot_receipt,build_snapshot_source_capture}.rs` for M3.7; `{runtime_session,runtime_session_receipt}.rs` for M3.8 | G3/G5, then run-output interface; never edit an A file concurrently. |
| C3 source providers | `source_{maven,git,decompile,archive,cache}.rs`, `dependency_sources.rs`, source-provider CLI/cache tests | M1.5/G9 and M2 root contract; M3.10 owns source-tree publication and the local-only cleanup guard. |
| C4 transformed sources/index | `jar_build/{engine_sources,source_output_filetree,source_identifier_remapper}.rs`, `java_analysis/dependency_index_{identity,store}.rs`, symbol consumers | M1.5/G9; M3.11 follows the source-provider interface without waiting for every Forge/remap slice; coordinate shared A-file edits. |
| D qualification | Applicability ledger/receipts; GameTest fixture source only on a separately justified bug | G6 freeze and integrated source; owns the serialized heavy lane. |

One writer per file; explicit handoff at overlap. Freeze milestone exit criteria before coding. A new correctness/safety blocker requires source evidence, a bounded repair and new proof for the affected slice; optional refinements go to the deferred backlog with owner. Do not silently defer correctness or let an optional improvement extend an already satisfied milestone.
M1.5/G9 precede source-provider cutover and M3.10/M3.11; M1.6/G10 is a bounded assessment and may close with all substitutions deferred. C3 and C4 can progress after their common root/source contracts are fixed, independently of asset/run integrity. M5 cannot claim source-analysis cache reuse until its selected source-provider and index rows have passed; optional replacement implementation never blocks M5 by itself.
M0.2/G11 closes before any parallel source mutation or Cargo/native validation. The integration coordinator owns every frozen window and transfers completed worker checkpoints only after quiescence; one heavy job remains the separate resource limit.

### [~] M1.1 Measure physical ownership and safe write budget

**2026-10-28 capacity checkpoint, not G1 completion:** Before the focused Cargo build C: had 106.1 GB and D: 56.7 GB free; afterward C: had 105.9 GB and D: 42.6 GB free. The first cold test profile consumed about 14.1 GB of D: headroom, so further builds require a fresh free-space/peak-write check. Prior bounded inventory measured 137.7 GB **logical** in generated projections and 22.3 MB allocated in a 45-JAR single-link sample; these are not a total physical baseline or savings claim. No old cache migration or cleanup occurred. Disjoint allocated categories, old-plus-new physical baseline, numerical reduction target and unchanged-run budget remain open.

**Measured category receipt, 2026-10-28:** The read-only `scripts/cache-storage-inventory.py` uses Win32 `FileStandardInfo.AllocationSize` plus handle-derived volume/file identity, skips descendant reparse points and needs no administrative privilege or content hashing. The corrected three-root receipt (`sfm-allocated-baseline-union-winid-20261028.json` in the task evidence directory) reports **175,138,098,976 unique allocated bytes** across the legacy C: cache, current D: toolchain root and generated projections, with zero read errors. The disjoint root allocations are C: 16,406,826,224 bytes (195,722 names, 76 hardlink aliases), D: 16,682,674,968 bytes (195,723 names, no aliases) and projections 142,048,597,784 bytes (2,381,951 names, no aliases). The latter has 137,686,713,407 logical bytes. The 56 projection-local `build/sfm-toolchain` trees counted 133,395,183,096 allocated bytes / 129,590,138,179 logical bytes across 2,060,158 files, nested inside the projection total; the corrected union established no hardlink aliases in the projection root, so their prior per-path allocation sum is also unique within that root. Their top categories are `native-project` 56.58 GiB, `nfrt` 38.88 GiB, `nfrt-invocations` 26.64 GiB, dependency remap 1.74 GiB and Forge preparation 0.37 GiB. One dev 1.19.2 `native-project` identity counted 684.8 MB allocated, including dependencies 233.8 MB, project 167.2 MB, Forge 138.9 MB, MCP 109.8 MB and run 34.8 MB. A same-MC Flux Networks JAR pair has equal 326 ZIP-entry payloads and 110 differing timestamps; this is a bounded duplicate witness, not a license to normalize every JAR. **Correction:** initial Python `st_ino`/`st_nlink` values missed Windows hardlinks and overstated the union by 289,161,216 bytes. The handle-derived two-link 3,000-byte test fixture correctly reports one 4,096-byte allocation; its independent copy requires a second allocation. The three-root total is a measured baseline for those roots only, not all SFM storage or recoverable bytes. At receipt time C: had 106,219,098,112 free bytes and D: 42,629,677,056; future writes require a fresh check. The numerical reclamation target and unchanged-run budget still require candidate ownership/retention evidence; no item is approved for deletion from this inventory.

These findings do not permit a cache cutover.

Thus neither an existing global `cache root set` value nor the derived-root override alone implements the approved ignored repo-local root for Rust **and** Gradle. Preserve existing roots and avoid a silent default switch until the G3/G7 contracts, relative Gradle path and cutover gates are validated.

**Later opt-in correction, 2026-10-28:** M2.1 now routes explicit native `--cache-root` downloads and derived project output through one guarded root; the preceding paragraph describes the original inventory boundary, not current opt-in behavior. The global cache remains the default without the option, Gradle requires its own property, historical native JARs remain separate, and the native root still rejects reparse links. No default cutover or old-cache migration occurred.

Until those runtime tests pass, M2.1 remains incomplete even though G7's design decision may already be closed.

**Completion criteria:** G3 contract and root-only link admission tests pass after the M2.3/approval prerequisites; old data and compiled historical roots are unchanged. A design decision alone does not complete runtime link support.

`source_git.rs` already implements one bare repository per canonical HTTPS remote, a per-repository fetch lock, exact commit verification, and separate commit-keyed source trees. `jar_build/resolve.rs` materializes a mutable producer checkout under a locked source-build root with a per-checkout lock; it is not a Git linked worktree. ## Overall terminal definition of done

- [ ] Checkout-local root approved and ignored; Rust resolves verified relative paths and strict locked dependencies; Gradle launches independently with pinned Java; plain 4.34/4.35 release source remains truthful. Gradle-user-home unification is deferred.
- [ ] Each worktree may select an approved isolated real root or an intentionally linked shared physical root at the same logical path; the findable worktree decision guide, physical-target reporting, root-only link admission and fail-closed negatives are proven. Creating test worktrees/links still requires separate approval.
- [ ] 74-key ledger and applicable 50-target core distinguish source/build/package/launch/GameTest. Required Rust correctness rows need current-source proof; Gradle-only forensic producer/consumer joins are deferred under the 2026-11-02 user scope change, while direct pinned-Java Gradle launch remains required. Unresolved required Rust correctness rows prevent completion.
- [ ] Frozen pins/runtime inputs remain validated; compatible projections physically reuse stages; relevant changes invalidate; corrupt/partial entries cannot be consumed.
- [ ] Every current-source build/test/runtime receipt names its coordinator, complete frozen input and tool/config identity, pre/post match and child/mutable-writer quiescence; drifted or interrupted runs are marked stale rather than counted.
- [ ] Source JARs, materialized source trees, transformed Minecraft/mod sources, mappings and symbol indexes have separate validated producer/reader/reachability contracts; `dependency source` and symbol lookup still work under shared-root cutover, while local-only Git-source cleanup cannot delete a shared owner's input.
- [ ] Build-tool JARs versus compile/runtime JARs retain the source-backed inventory already obtained; additional Gradle forensic closure and native replacement experiments are deferred unless needed for a concrete normal-launch failure. Optional rewrites are not required for Rust cache storage closure or applied to historical plain recipes.
- [ ] Future successful scratch and repeated unchanged **build and run** growth meet measured bounds; worlds/options/evidence remain intact.
- [ ] Separately approved selective retirement achieves measured **substantial allocated-byte reduction across old plus new SFM roots**, meeting reviewed G1 numerical target. Cutover without this is incomplete.
- [ ] Report-only expected-versus-existing candidates distinguish checkout-local expectation from global ownership; any shared retirement has complete owner-union, active-reader and exact physical-identity proof plus separate approval. Shared physical targets count once in the savings ledger.
- [ ] Final tool freshness, process state, manual commands, rollback state, commits and deferred optional work are reported; no unauthorized release/push.

## Risk register and deferred backlog

| Risk | Control |
| --- | --- |
| Moved absolute receipts falsely pass | G3/M5 path/schema checks; regenerate incompatible receipts. |
| Shared bytes mutated by a loader/Gradle/consumer | G2 consumer audit, held read-only input or proven necessary local copy. |
| Disk peak or wrong cleanup harms world/evidence | G1 capacity stop, M6 exact approval and owner checks. |
| Cache hit hides wrong bytes/missing source | Frozen outer check, asset/output digest negatives, cold/offline repeat. |
| Gradle silently requires Rust | G4 independent invocation and bounded shared-artifact scope. |
| Unrelated edit invalidates all preparation | G2 stage keys and unrelated-source witness. |
| Sessions grow despite preparation reuse | M3.8 unchanged-run allocated-byte proof. |
| Broken/misdirected link admits another owner's cache or a mutable tree | G7 target identity/marker/capacity checks, narrow root-only admission and fail-closed tests; no automatic links. |
| One checkout incorrectly prunes a resource needed by another | G8/M3.9 candidates only; M6 owner union, active-reader/quiescence and exact approval. |
| Logical path aliases inflate claimed savings or split per-key locks | G1 counts canonical physical targets once; G7/M3 prove a common lock and publication namespace. |
| Decompiled/extracted source tree is trusted from marker alone or removed under a live search/index reader | G9/M3.10 output inventory, held reader and interrupted-repair tests; M3.11 checks namespace and index payload. |
| Local Git-source cleanup prunes another linked worktree's source | G8 owner-union/quiescence gate; disable/refuse `dependency source cache cleanup --confirm` on shared roots until proven. |
| Native tool replacement changes mappings or historic JAR output | G10 rank/compare per producer; optional experiment only with versioned semantic/output and provenance proof. |
| Endless optional refinements | Freeze exits; defer nonblocking item with owner and separate approval. |
| Parallel edits or a test writer make a Cargo/native result stale despite a target/output lock | G11/M0.2 source-stable window, separate mutable-effect lease, pre/post identity and stale-result recovery. |

**Deferred, not terminal blockers unless a gate exposes a correctness failure:** per-producer deterministic ZIP normalization; native Rust replacement of ForgeFlower/Vineflower/FART/SpecialSource/DiffPatch/ANTLR tooling after G10 assessment; automatic cross-worktree sharing or auto-pruning (neither requested); opaque cross-version byte deduplication; version-specific Gradle file pruning; eventual 4.34 baseline retirement; Prism/tracked-server internal cache redesign; unrelated release-report and localization changes. Each needs a separate scoped decision after this plan closes.

### Bounded gate diagnosis, 2026-10-28

**Source-only residual Clippy triage (no new compilation):** The current 29 strict diagnostic sites are in `inspection_cli` (7), `forge_preparation_cache` (6), `engine_run` (4), `run_client_cli` (3), `build_snapshot_receipt` (3), `dependency_remap_cache` (3), and one each in `engine_execute`, `forge_preparation_inputs` and `catalog_owned_project`. The Forge/remap and snapshot long-function/argument sites straddle publication, held-reader lifetime and mutable projection output, so splitting them needs focused integrity tests. The one `forge_preparation_inputs` site is in a currently unrelated dirty file and is not ours to format or stage. Small mechanical suggestions (`Option::default`, an unfulfilled lint expectation, a redundant closure and status-only boolean conversion) are candidates for a later frozen-source, tested lint repair; none was edited in this read-only pass. The unused snapshot/catalog methods need call-graph and compatibility review before removal. No residual diagnostic was silently waived or classified as harmless.

## Fail-closed Gradle producer boundary, 2026-10-29

The missing-output preflight now requires `SFM_PRODUCER_EXTERNAL_INPUTS_SHA256`; a valid historical receipt alone produces an explicit missing-manifest error. The resolver still refuses after candidate verification, before checkout, output lock or Gradle. The direct Gradle source-build entry point also rejects a producer-pinned recipe before checkout, so a caller cannot bypass that resolver refusal through the general explicit-source builder. Ordinary unpinned explicit-source builds retain their existing behavior.

Focused tests passed: 6/6 receipt, 3/3 external-manifest, 5/5 producer-native with one retained-output fixture ignored, and 1/1 direct-Gradle no-effect regression. The latter and producer-native test commands had the same pre/post crate dirty-content identity `a99edc6421cc214f6be5d551a9ae8facc7f4c30655cd73f8af457a3dc8a5655a`. No real Mekanism producer ran and no output hashes changed. This closes only the bypass-prevention slice. The exact external manifest, offline read isolation for wrapper/plugin/buildSrc/configuration/task-time inputs, unknown-read negative witness and both frozen JAR output checks remain required before missing-output admission. A generic Gradle `--offline` flag by itself would not prove task-time read closure.

**2026-11-02 per-context held Forge SDK image and normal Rust launch latency:** An ExecutionContext now captures the selected local Forge JBR SDK image once and shares the same held, exact file/directory handles across MCP, Forge and dependency-remap input authorities in that context. Every subsequent borrow rechecks selected membership; existing held-file write/delete exclusion, strict pins, source checks, cancellation and output verification remain active. A new synthetic regression verifies one image is reused and that an added SDK member is rejected. The first normal dev 1.19.2 client m699 exited 0/sourceStable true in 7m50s after the changed recipe source selected a new producer key; m700 exited 0/sourceStable true in 4m42s but ran main, GameTest and datagen javac again, so neither is credited as an unchanged compiler-cache repeat. Same-CLI/source m701 and m702 both exited 0/sourceStable true, reached the title screen, reported zero network attempts and reused all three javac outputs. Their whole-command times were **3m49s and 3m48s**, versus m694's prior **4m27s** warm baseline; m701's javac argfile and input state were byte-identical before/after. In m702, build preparation was 39.4s versus m694's 48.7s, runtime Forge/MCP rehold 21.4s versus 25.8s, and classpath resolution 17.4s versus 25.7s. These are non-overlapping stages; the remaining wall difference also includes uninstrumented/system variation, so it is not all attributed to the code edit. Session materialization remains about 35.7s, including 21.7s of private overlay copies, and Minecraft startup about 45.7s. The final D: free reading was 1,681,596,416 bytes; no new archive relocation or permanent retirement occurred, and this is not a physical-savings claim. Receipts: action-services-evidence/m696-held-sdk-context-test.run.json, m697-held-sdk-forge-tests.run.json, m698-held-sdk-context-cli-build.run.json, and m699 through m702 held-SDK client run JSON/trace files. The benchmarked binary was built from the exact dirty-content source identity recorded in those receipts; unrelated pre-existing Forge formatting and report edits were preserved rather than silently staged. Multi-minute unchanged launch latency still remains.


**2026-11-02 same-invocation Forge preparation authority handoff:** For an ordinary Forge run, the build and runtime now share one ExecutionContext, and the build retains both completed MCP/Forge shared-output holds and their captured producer-input owners through runtime prelaunch capture. The input owners are rechecked, the path-only Minecraft library cache is invalidated, and producer input handles are released before the child JVM starts; shared output readers remain held through the runtime consumer. Immediately before using those shared paths, runtime rechecks both owners; if either complete authority is unavailable it takes the previous fresh-admission route. The MCP owner includes the selected authenticated Minecraft library files. File handles keep selected payloads read-only and undeletable during use; the existing remap and runtime classpath checks remain separate. The non-run build entrypoint retains its prior API and lifetime. Runtime MCP/Forge preparation admission fell from 21.4s in m702 to 0.22s in both repeats; JVM startup varied from 46 to 50s, so the smaller whole-command improvement is reported separately. The postlaunch timer now excludes JVM wait and measured 2.3–2.6s. m708's nonoverlapping wall components were projection/source identity 15.6s, native plan resolution 39.4s, reused build 40.3s, snapshot 7.5s, runtime prelaunch 58.2s (including session materialization 37.0s and classpath resolution 17.6s), JVM wait 46.4s, and postlaunch 2.3s. This leaves multi-minute latency, especially fresh-process exact SDK/plan checks, remap/build verification, and private session materialization. No persistent timestamp-only trust, exact-lock waiver, archive purge, or physical-saving claim was made. Evidence: action-services-evidence/m703-held-forge-handoff-tests.frozen.json.run.json, m704-held-forge-handoff-build.frozen.json.run.json, and m705–m708 client run JSON/trace files. D: free after m708 was 1,442,021,376 bytes; no further archive offload occurred.


**Final prelaunch-owner release qualification:** Holding the producer's source and tool files for the entire interactive client would block normal edits. The final code therefore rechecks and releases MCP/Forge producer-input owners after private launch-input capture, and clears the execution context's path-only Minecraft library cache at that boundary. This preserves held shared-output readers and all independent runtime remap/classpath checks. Source-stable m716 passed the new path-cache invalidation regression (1/1), m717 passed the 56 Forge input/output/corruption tests, and m718 built the final offline locked CLI, SHA-256 9205D2118C6ED44CAF52C63836948E07765B29AA89B94393C1CDC7A048754D37. Normal no-override client m719 and m720 both exited 0/sourceStable true with this exact CLI, all three javac sets reused, zero network attempts, title screen reached, no lingering owned process, and complete command times **3m29s and 3m29s**. In both runs the retained runtime preparation recheck was 0.22s and prelaunch producer-input release/recheck was 0.22s, versus 21.4s for runtime preparation admission in m702. m720's nonoverlapping stages were source/projection identity about 15.6s, native plan resolution about 39.4s, reused build 40.1s, snapshot about 7.5s, runtime prelaunch 58.1s (including session materialization 37.0s and classpath resolution 17.3s), child wait 45.7s, and postlaunch 2.3s. The baseline m701/m702 was 3m49s/3m48s; the patch is a measured roughly 19–20 second end-to-end reduction on this machine, not an acceptable final latency target. No old data was deleted, and D: free after m720 was 2,054,922,240 bytes. Evidence: action-services-evidence/m716-held-forge-release-test.frozen.json.run.json, m717-held-forge-release-suite.frozen.json.run.json, m718-held-forge-release-build.frozen.json.run.json, and m719/m720 held-forge-released client receipt/trace pairs.

**2026-11-02 managed JBR seal and explicit audit:** Normal Rust launch now treats a completed, checked JBR archive/install as immutable managed cache state. Under the per-artifact writer lock, first acquisition or a missing completion seal still verifies the pinned archive SHA-512 and every extracted file before publishing a versioned create-only seal. A sealed hit checks the selected pin/receipt, required real paths and bounded held archive, then retains selected launch-file handles; it does not recursively hash the archive or installed tree on every process. SFM writers never mutate a sealed entry. This trusts that users do not manually edit managed toolchain bytes between runs; an external change outside selected held paths may be found only by explicit audit. Source changes, exact lock selection, writer coordination, crash-before-seal recovery and reader holds remain enforced. The new read-only cache verify-jdk command performs the full archive and installed-tree comparison, with no repair/delete; a live audit of sfm-dev/mc-1.19.2 verified one pinned pair. Source-stable m730 passed 17 focused JBR tests (one large official round trip intentionally ignored), m732 passed the CLI parser test, and m736 built the offline locked CLI, SHA-256 6F68F227DA8D49BE8C4040C32C4D5221ECD804B0F9D8D5D7C362679E9A996C60. Normal no-Java/cache-override clients m737–m739 all reached title screen, exited 0/sourceStable true, reused all three javac outputs, made zero network attempts and left no task-owned process. First seal creation m737 took 4m19s with 41.4s native plan resolution; two separate sealed warm runs m738/m739 took 3m12.6s/3m08.3s with 1.75s/1.79s native plan resolution. The prior full-audit warm model m719/m720 took 3m29s/3m28.6s: the stage reduction is about 39.6s, while end-to-end improvement observed here is about 16–20s; other wall-time variation is not attributed to this edit. Multi-minute latency remains, and normal Forge SDK image capture still hashes about 206 MB per independent launch. The 2026-11-02 two-root verified inactive-toolchain archive raised D: free from 690,282,496 to 1,959,006,208 bytes, retaining the full hierarchy and restoration manifests under G:; this is 1,268,723,712 bytes of D: headroom, not net physical savings. No old archive was purged. Whole-file rustfmt was auto-review rejected because it would alter unrelated formatting; focused code and parser tests plus an offline build passed, and unrelated dirty files remain untouched.

**2026-11-02 managed dependency-remap receipt and repeat latency:** A two-output remap entry first passes the existing full output-inventory comparison under its per-key writer lock; only then is a versioned create-only seal published. A later held-shared hit checks the selected input fingerprint, small manifest digest/schema, exact two output names and sizes, safe regular files and held remapped-JAR ancestry without recursively hashing both JARs. This follows the user's explicit managed-cache trust model: SFM never mutates a completed entry, while external same-size edits between runs are trusted until an explicit full verification. The original full project_compilation_cache::matches verifier remains for unsealed adoption and non-direct restore; a user-facing all-remaps doctor/repair command is still open. Wrong fingerprint, malformed/missing seal, missing payload and interrupted publication refuse fast reuse; pinned original, mapping, tool and SDK input checks and writer/reader holds remain. The first m743 broad suite exposed a real first-seal ordering error: a directory reader lease blocked seal creation. It was corrected by full verification and seal publication before acquiring the selected payload hold. Source-stable m745 and final-source m750 each passed 55/55 Forge/remap regressions; m746 built the offline locked CLI SHA-256 F7E9FF88FA1D49D03CE8D4D600608A6BE924DEC4F7FCD07D268CE417983FFF39. Normal dev 1.19.2 no-override client m747 first-adoption, m748 and m749 unchanged repeats all exited 0/sourceStable true, reached title screen, reused shared remaps and three javac sets, made zero network attempts, and left no task-owned process. Whole-command times were 168.818s, 168.781s and 171.651s versus same-projection prior sealed-JBR-only m739's 188.345s; the observed improvement is roughly 17–20s against that single prior run, not a universal guarantee. Build stage was 32.1–33.0s on m748/m749 versus 39.2s in m739. Repeated project-compilation output-inventory events dropped from 129 in m739 to 43 during m747 adoption and 4 in each sealed repeat; overlapping nested event counts are not summed as elapsed time. m749 still spent about 50.3s in runtime prelaunch (including 35.2s session materialization and 11.4s classpath resolution), 44.3s waiting for the Minecraft title-screen smoke, about 33.0s in reused build and additional source/projection/snapshot checks. The ~2m49–2m52 unchanged launch remains above the user's acceptable latency and is an open usability gate. No old archive purge, global install or net physical-saving claim follows from this latency work. The five unrelated release-template edits and other pre-existing dirty files were not staged.

**2026-11-02 explicit full managed-remap audit and capacity recovery:** `cache verify-remaps --project-root <catalogued projection>` now checks every owned `dependency-remap-v1` recipe key under its producer lock, validates the exact two-output schema and seal, and runs the retained full output-inventory hash comparison. `--key <64-hex>` permits a bounded single-recipe audit. The command reports incomplete or damaged entries and fails without sealing, repairing or deleting them; the producer's `work` scratch root is explicitly excluded, while unknown store children are rejected. Focused tests passed for a complete unsealed entry, changed output bytes, forged seal, interrupted publication, expected scratch and unknown-child refusal; the parser test passed. The checkout-local CLI SHA-256 `1C672B42C393B5F4E411E5CE433A9EA2193491E49209AD4D4BD1A0D760EED335` audited one key, then full-store receipt `action-services-evidence/m762-full-remap-audit-20261102.out` (SHA-256 `5AA4CD639F30B48D4DCCD8D1370E27651BC6A15C4DB102945BFD50B84520E1CC`) reported **391 keys, 391 verified completed entries, zero incomplete, zero damaged, one producer scratch root excluded**. The first full attempt m761 stopped at that known scratch root without inspecting payloads or changing cache data; scanner classification was corrected and retested. This audit is the expensive counterpart to trusted warm reuse, not a repeated launch preflight or repair operation. It audits completed remap outputs; it does not independently establish every current projection's selected producer-input lock closure. The earlier m747 client was first-use remap sealing; m748 and m749 were the independent unchanged repeats at 168.781s and 171.651s. The remaining 33-second reused-build and 35-second private-session stages remain open; `BuildSnapshotReceipt` currently performs full snapshot inventory on reuse and around materialization, plus private copy/destination inventory. A safe next slice is a tool-owned completion and reader contract for immutable compiled snapshots with editable-source freshness separately checked, followed by measured normal client repeats. No such session reuse optimization or new latency claim is made by this audit commit.

**2026-11-02 managed successful-build snapshot fast-path check:** Fresh snapshot publication still verifies the full BLAKE3 output inventory before publishing `latest-successful-build.json` under the project build lock. **2026-11-02 held shared Forge runtime snapshot:** Ordinary Windows Forge `run client` and `run client --smoke` now consume a completed snapshot's distinct resource/class roots directly through Forge `MOD_CLASSES`. A per-invocation owner holds each selected file against write/delete and the directory ancestry against replacement until the JVM exits; it checks exact snapshot entry membership afterward. It trusts payload bytes sealed at snapshot publication under the managed-cache contract. Explicit full `BuildSnapshotReceipt::recheck()` remains the external-corruption audit. Name collisions across roots (case-folded on Windows), missing entries, unsupported shapes and unavailable reader leases do not admit direct consumption; they use the checked private materializer or fail. NeoForge, server/test/data/puppet and JDWP hotswap sessions retain their established private path. The normal client's private slot still owns mutable world/options/logs and launch inputs; this change does not share writable session data. Four focused direct-reader tests passed for held writes/renames, added-name detection, case-folded collision/missing-entry fallback, empty private mod with private state, and the normal four-root set. One route-eligibility test passed for Forge client/smoke and negative hotswap/NeoForge/server cases; the existing 25 snapshot tests passed before the final guard edit.

The first checked live run `m780` intentionally took the private fallback because it had the normal four roots before that shape was admitted. `m781` selected the direct path and reached the title screen. `m782` was interrupted during SDK verification by the announced Codex restart, before Minecraft launch, and has no terminal run receipt. `m783/m784` then passed as two unchanged direct-path runs (122.578s/125.890s CLI operation) on the pre-hotswap-guard source. Direct snapshot materialization was 0ms in both final runs versus 29.668s/29.113s private copy; prelaunch was 18.660s/17.719s versus 45.376s/45.152s. JVM wait varied upward to 56.831s/54.054s versus 49.777s/48.820s, so the observed end-to-end gain is smaller than the removed copy cost. These spans overlap and must not be summed.
